| 5f0e5e653d843c034d78e0bd866a5bfb4f5f4e59^ | 465 | Output VAT sits in 44571, input VAT on expenses in 44566 and on capital assets in 44562, the balance payable in 44551. Reconcile them to the return every period and clear 44551 when the payment leaves the bank. The normal regime files a CA3 return, the simplified regime one annual CA12 return with the two instalments above; boxes and mechanics are in `france-vat-return`. A franchised business has no output VAT and nothing to reclaim, which is why its bookkeeping is a purchases register and a receipts journal rather than VAT accounts. | Actual current components: LF469 Output VAT sits in44571, expense input VAT in44566, capital-asset input VAT in44562 and the payable balance in44551. Reconcile each reporting period and clear44551 against payment. The qualified CA3/CA12 and instalment mechanics are in `france-vat-return`. A domestic-sales franchise removes collection and associated input deduction for those operations; it does not remove commercial books, nor every VAT duty on foreign purchases. Keep the actual taxable-purchase and option analysis.; Historical disposition: Reconcile each reporting period and clear44551 against payment.. source-qualified successor |

| 5f0e5e653d843c034d78e0bd866a5bfb4f5f4e59^ | 484 | Four more particulars arrive with electronic invoicing: the client's Siren number when the client is a business, the delivery address when it differs, the nature of the operations (goods, services or both), and the wording for the option to pay the tax on the debits. Every business established in France and liable to VAT must be able to receive electronic invoices since 1 September 2026; issuing starts then for large and intermediate companies and on 1 September 2027 for small, medium and micro businesses. See `france-einvoice`. Special wordings cover membership of an approved management body, the VAT franchise, "Auto-liquidation" where the customer is liable for the VAT, the electrical waste contribution, the private copying levy, "Auto-facturation" where the client raises the invoice, and a craft business's professional insurance references. | Actual current components: LF488 Four additional particulars follow the applicable electronic-invoicing issue deadline: business customer Siren, different goods-delivery address, goods/services/both classification and the specified debit-option wording. French VAT taxable persons, including franchise businesses and persons making only exempt sales, must be able to receive in-scope electronic invoices from1September2026. Issuing/data transmission starts then for large/intermediate entities, with the statutory1September2027 extension for PME/micro entities except VAT-group members. Size is tested at legal-unit level using1January2025 and the stated accounts reference, not the transaction-year label alone. See `france-einvoice`. Other conditional mentions include approved-body membership, franchise, “Auto-liquidation”, electrical-waste contribution, private-copying levy, “Auto-facturation” and applicable craft professional-insurance references; each needs its actual scope.; Historical disposition: source-qualified successor |
| 5f0e5e653d843c034d78e0bd866a5bfb4f5f4e59^ | 486 | ## The fichier des écritures comptables | Actual current components: LF490 ## The fichier des écritures comptables; Historical disposition: carried exact |
| 5f0e5e653d843c034d78e0bd866a5bfb4f5f4e59^ | 488 | A business that keeps its books on a computer hands the inspector a copy of the accounting entries file at the start of a tax audit, in the format set by article A. 47 A-1 of the livre des procédures fiscales. It concerns taxpayers who keep computerised accounts, must hold and produce accounting documents under the CGI, and are audited: corporation tax payers, and income tax payers under a real regime for industrial and commercial, non-commercial or agricultural profits. A property company is released only where it is taxed exclusively on rental income and all of its members are individuals. Any other property company must produce the file. A micro-entrepreneur who keeps the registers on a computer is also released. See https://bofip.impots.gouv.fr/bofip/9026-PGP.html and https://bofip.impots.gouv.fr/bofip/9028-PGP.html | Actual current components: LF492 A taxpayer keeping computerised books, subject to CGI accounting-document duties and undergoing a comptabilité audit, provides its FEC at the start of control in the prescribed format. This includes IS and real-regime BIC/BNC/BA taxpayers under the actual conditions. BOI9026’s tolerance exempts SCI taxed exclusively as revenus fonciers with only natural-person members, but SCI meeting Code de commerce L612-1/R612-1 still provide an FEC regardless of tax regime; the tolerance does not exempt the other SCI described there. Computerised auto-entrepreneur registers have the stated separate tolerance. See https://bofip.impots.gouv.fr/bofip/9026-PGP.html and https://bofip.impots.gouv.fr/bofip/9028-PGP.html; Historical disposition: source-qualified successor |

| 11eb01a4 | 60 | \| 201 \| Frais d'établissement \| Formation costs (may capitalise or expense) \| | Actual current components: LF93 \| 201 \| Frais d'établissement \|; LF104 \| 29 \| Dépréciations des immobilisations \|; LF106 **Formation costs, the retained accounting choice.** Under PCG212-9, expenditure conditioning the entity's existence or overall development that cannot be linked to specific goods or services follows the stated route: constitution, transformation and first-establishment costs may be capitalised as frais d'établissement, but recognition in profit or loss is the reference method. Capitalised frais d'établissement are amortised under a plan within five years. This restores the original account201 topic; it is not the EUR500 equipment tolerance or a universal permission to capitalise operating costs. The same article gives capital-increase, merger and division costs their distinct capitalisation/share-premium route, with any premium shortfall expensed. Source: https://www.anc.gouv.fr/files/anc/files/1_Normes_fran%C3%A7aises/recueil/2026/Recueil-PCG-Janvier-2026.pdf; Historical disposition: R99 supported accounting choice is now restored adjacent to account201: reference expensing or permitted capitalisation underPCG212-9, not an unsupported historical claim. |

| 11eb01a4 | 152 | \| 77 \| Produits exceptionnels \| Exceptional income (asset disposals) \| | Actual current components: LF180 \| 757 \| Produits des cessions d'immobilisations incorporelles et corporelles \|; LF183 \| 77 \| Produits exceptionnels \|; LF282 The compte de résultat gathers the income and expenses of the year and shows the profit or loss after depreciation and provisions. Règlement 2022-06 of 4 November 2022 moved accounts that fed the exceptional result into the operating result, and binds financial years beginning on or after 1 January 2025. What stays in the exceptional result is income and expenses directly linked to a major and unusual event, entries of purely tax origin such as amortissements dérogatoires and provisions réglementées, a change of accounting method taken through profit because of a tax rule, and corrections of errors other than those taken straight to equity. An event is unusual when it is not tied to the entity's normal and current activity. An event is presumed unusual when it has not happened in recent years and is unlikely to happen again. See https://bofip.impots.gouv.fr/bofip/13223-PGP.html and https://entreprendre.service-public.fr/vosdroits/F21852; Historical disposition: Règlement 2022-06 of 4 November 2022 moved accoun. Historical wording is preserved; operative current conditions and dates govern. |

| 11eb01a4 | 209 | \| **Fiscal tolerance (biens de faible valeur)** \| ≤ EUR 500 HT per item \| May be expensed directly (small equipment, office supplies, tools, software) \| | Actual current components: LF242 \| Unit price excluding tax up to which materials and tooling may be deducted at once \| EUR 500 \| "n'excédant pas 500 €" \|; LF244 The tax tolerance covers qualifying materials/tooling in2154/2155, small office equipment, office/shop furniture and software whose unit value excluding tax does not exceed EUR500. Transport equipment is excluded from the tooling limb; connected components are assessed together. Furniture normally qualifies for limited renewal purchases of the same item; initial furnishings or a complete renewal qualify only if their total price does not exceed EUR500 excluding tax. These are category-specific tax conditions, not a universal accounting threshold. PCG212-1 separately requires probable future economic benefits and reliable cost measurement, and212-6 permits nonsignificant assets to be expensed. An otherwise qualifying small item can still be capitalised.; Historical disposition: Historical wording is preserved; operative current conditions and dates govern. |

| 11eb01a4 | 232 | Available for new assets with useful life ≥ 3 years (not buildings, vehicles, or furniture). Coefficients applied to straight-line rate: | Actual current components: LF269 An asset qualifies only if it falls in the categories set by article 22 of annexe II to the CGI and by article 39 A of the CGI, was not already second hand when bought, and has a normal useful life of at least three years. See https://bofip.impots.gouv.fr/bofip/4684-PGP.html and https://bofip.impots.gouv.fr/bofip/4682-PGP.html; LF278 For ordinary qualifying acquisitions from1January2010, use the coefficients above; the source separately preserves older acquisition-date bands. Multiply the linear rate for the normal useful life by the coefficient. Start at the first day of the acquisition/construction month with financial-period proration; switch to residual value divided by remaining years where that produces the greater permitted charge. Special equipment coefficients require their own provision. The general exclusions for dwellings/worksites/professional premises must be tested with the actual statutory categories: qualifying industrial buildings with normal useful life no more than fifteen years are a named exception. The hotel limb covers businesses providing accommodation or accommodation with meals, not restaurants/cafés alone, clinics, private retirement-home buildings or camping investment merely because they provide lodging. Newness, ownership and incorporation/separability conditions still apply, especially work on old or rented hotel buildings. Used assets are excluded unless an actual stated exception applies; the manufacturer-rebuilt tolerance requires latest technical improvements and a price approaching new equipment. Do not extend that tolerance to any refurbished purchase. If accounting depreciation is linear, record the tax excess as amortissements dérogatoires.; Historical disposition: Multiply the linear rate for the no. Historical wording is preserved; operative current conditions and dates govern. |

| 11eb01a4 | 361 | \| Bookkeeping \| Cash receipts/payments journal only; no double-entry required \| Full double-entry; simplified liasse \| Full double-entry \| | Actual current components: LF51 The general commercial books are the livre-journal and grand-livre, with procedural documentation when needed. Entries identify their origin, content, account and supporting document. Cotation and paraphe by the greffier are available at the merchant’s request under Code de commerce R123-173; the retained administrative page states them as compulsory, and that broader wording is not adopted. Electronic records must support identification, date and integrity; validated computer entries are immutable. The ordinary auxiliary-journal centralisation rule is at least monthly. L123-25 permits qualifying natural persons under the simplified real income-tax regime to record receipts/payments during the year and receivables/payables at close, without an annexe. It separately permits qualifying commercial legal persons to use an abridged annexe. The additional permission to record receivables/payables only at close excludes entities controlled by a parent preparing consolidated accounts under L233-16; that exclusion belongs to the closing-recording permission, not automatically to the abridged annexe. L123-26 allows natural persons under the simplified real income-tax regime, by option or by right, to recognise expenses in profit or loss according to their payment date where the expense recurs at intervals no longer than one year, excluding purchases. This is a qualifying expense/payment-date rule, not permission to defer every general-expense posting to an annual entry; L123-27 permits simplified stock/work-in-progress valuation. The Service Public sole-trader branch describes quarterly centralisation and an annual super-simplified election on2031. Its exact bridge to the ordinary monthly rule is not established here: do not apply quarterly centralisation to every company or infer the income-tax regime from VAT liability.; LF53 For the natural persons within Code de commerce L123-28 and CGI50-0, the commercial-law relief replaces annual accounts with the prescribed receipts and, where relevant, purchase records. Do not apply that legal-form-specific relief to every entity described as “micro”.; LF431 \| Bookkeeping \| Natural-person micro relief: prescribed receipts/purchases records \| Journal/ledger; documentation when needed; qualifying L123-25 relief and sole-trader election above \| Journal/ledger, documentation when needed; ordinary chronological entries \|; LF432 \| Centralisation \| No double-entry centralisation under the specified register relief \| Ordinary rule at least monthly; administrative sole-trader quarterly branch remains specifically qualified above \| At least monthly for auxiliary journals \|; Historical disposition: Entries identify their origin, content, account and supporting docume; LF53 For the natural persons within Code de commerce L123-28 and CGI50-0, the commercial-law relief replaces annual accounts with the prescribed receipts and, where relevant, purchase r; LF430 \| Bookkeeping \| Natural-person micro relief: prescribed receipts/purchases records \| Journal/ledger; documentation when needed; qualifying L123-25 relief and sole-trader election a; LF431 \| Centralisation \| No double-entry centralisation under the specified register relief \| Ordinary rule at least monthly; administrative sole-trader quarterly branch remains specific. Historical wording is preserved; operative current conditions and dates govern. |
| 11eb01a4 | 362 | \| Financial statements \| None required \| Simplified bilan + compte de résultat \| Full bilan + compte de résultat + annexe \| | Actual current components: LF433 \| Annual accounts \| No annual accounts only for the specified L123-28 natural persons \| Natural-person L123-25 no-annexe rule; legal-person abridged-annexe conditions; actual presentation options \| Bilan, compte de résultat and annexe, subject to any separate applicable exemption \|; Historical disposition: Historical wording is preserved; operative current conditions and dates govern. |
| 11eb01a4 | 363 | \| TVA \| Franchise en base (no TVA charged, no returns) \| Annual TVA + 2 interim payments \| Monthly TVA declarations \| | Actual current components: LF434 \| VAT \| Independent franchise, option and foreign-purchase tests \| Independent simplified-VAT conditions and qualified CA12/instalment route \| Normal VAT reporting where its conditions apply; a VAT label does not determine the books \|; LF450 The ordinary domestic franchise tests both periods separately: the previous calendar year must satisfy its basic ceiling, and a current-year overrun of the higher ceiling ends the franchise from that overrun date. Exceeding the previous-year basic ceiling removes the franchise from1January of the following year; satisfying only the current higher ceiling does not restore it. Startup annualisation/source conflicts remain explicitly in `france-vat-return`. | 11eb01a4 | 366 | \| Annexe comptable \| Not required \| Simplified \| Full \| | Actual current components: LF51 The general commercial books are the livre-journal and grand-livre, with procedural documentation when needed. Entries identify their origin, content, account and supporting document. Cotation and paraphe by the greffier are available at the merchant’s request under Code de commerce R123-173; the retained administrative page states them as compulsory, and that broader wording is not adopted. Electronic records must support identification, date and integrity; validated computer entries are immutable. The ordinary auxiliary-journal centralisation rule is at least monthly. L123-25 permits qualifying natural persons under the simplified real income-tax regime to record receipts/payments during the year and receivables/payables at close, without an annexe. It separately permits qualifying commercial legal persons to use an abridged annexe. The additional permission to record receivables/payables only at close excludes entities controlled by a parent preparing consolidated accounts under L233-16; that exclusion belongs to the closing-recording permission, not automatically to the abridged annexe. L123-26 allows natural persons under the simplified real income-tax regime, by option or by right, to recognise expenses in profit or loss according to their payment date where the expense recurs at intervals no longer than one year, excluding purchases. This is a qualifying expense/payment-date rule, not permission to defer every general-expense posting to an annual entry; L123-27 permits simplified stock/work-in-progress valuation. The Service Public sole-trader branch describes quarterly centralisation and an annual super-simplified election on2031. Its exact bridge to the ordinary monthly rule is not established here: do not apply quarterly centralisation to every company or infer the income-tax regime from VAT liability.; LF53 For the natural persons within Code de commerce L123-28 and CGI50-0, the commercial-law relief replaces annual accounts with the prescribed receipts and, where relevant, purchase records.
